    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-thm-mean-cost",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-transfer-fixed-partition-moments"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-mc-transfer-2",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-mc-transfer</summary>\n\n\n*Proof of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:mean-cost\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:mean-cost\">9</a>.* The partition and costs are fixed throughout the proof. Choose $$0<\\delta<(V-c)/2,\n \\qquad 1<\\lambda<\\frac{V-\\delta}{c+\\delta}.$$ For any centered, variance-one law $Z$, use independent copies to define $$S_Z=\\sum_{i=1}^q Z_i,\\qquad L=\\mathcal P(\\mathbf Z),\\qquad\n V_Z=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(\\mathbb E[S_Z\\mid L]),\\qquad c_Z=\\mathbb E\\ell(L).$$ For a finite observation $F$ with $v=v(F)>0$, write $Z=T_F/\\sqrt v$. We will increase $v/D$ while maintaining $$\\label{eq:invariant}\n V_Z>V-\\delta,\\qquad c_Z<c+\\delta/2,\\qquad \\mathbb E|Z|>1/2.$$ Lemma <a href=\"#lem:mc-transfer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:mc-transfer\">10</a> shows that all three inequalities hold eventually along every sequence of centered, variance-one laws converging to a standard normal.\n\nTo initialize, observe the bit sum itself: $F=H_N$, with score $T_F=H_N$ and $v=D=N$. The central limit theorem for independent uniform signs [[Durrett](#ref-Durrett), Theorem 3.4.1] supplies a finite $N$ for which <a href=\"#eq:invariant\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:invariant\">[eq:invariant]</a> holds. The initial ratio is $v/D=1$.\n\n\nNow hold one such $F$ fixed. Apply $\\mathcal P$ to the standardized scores of $q$ independent copies of $F$, obtaining the group label $L$. The algebraic assumption and the degree calculus give $$\\deg(\\mathbf 1_{\\{L=l\\}})\\le D\\ell(l).$$ The tower property identifies the group score and its variance as $$\\label{eq:group}\n T_L=\\sqrt v\\,\\mathbb E[S_Z\\mid L],\\qquad\n \\tau^2:=v(L)=vV_Z>0.$$ Thus this group has a variance advantage relative to its mean cost. We next obtain a common degree bound without losing that advantage.\n\n\nTake $K$ independent copies of $L$. Retain a label word $(l_1,\\ldots,l_K)$ when $\\sum_{b=1}^K\\ell(l_b)\\le K(c+\\delta)$. Let $F'_K$ report each retained word exactly and report one failure label for every other word. Every retained-word indicator is a product with degree at most $$\\label{eq:degree}\n D'_K=DK(c+\\delta).$$ The failure indicator is one minus the finite sum of all retained-word indicators, so it satisfies the same degree bound on every input. The real upper bound $D'_K$ need not be rounded to an integer. Since the independent costs have mean $c_Z<c+\\delta/2$, the law of large numbers [[Durrett](#ref-Durrett), Theorem 2.2.3] gives a failure probability $\\varepsilon_K\\to0$.\n\n\nWrite $U_K=\\sum_{b=1}^KT_{L_b}$ and $T'_K=T_{F'_K}$. The tower property again gives $T'_K=\\mathbb E[U_K\\mid F'_K]$. On a retained word, $U_K$ is known exactly. On failure, its conditional mean is at least as good a predictor as zero [[Durrett](#ref-Durrett), Theorem 4.1.15]. Consequently $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E(U_K-T'_K)^2\n &\\le\\mathbb E[U_K^2\\mathbf 1_{\\{\\mathrm{failure}\\}}]\\notag\\\\\n &\\le(\\mathbb EU_K^4)^{1/2}\\varepsilon_K^{1/2}\n   =o(K\\tau^2). \\label{eq:loss}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For the last equality, $T_L$ has finite range, mean zero, and positive variance, and independence gives $$\\mathbb EU_K^4=K\\mathbb ET_L^4+3K(K-1)\\tau^4=O(K^2\\tau^4).$$ The implicit constant can depend on the current observation, which has been fixed before $K$ tends to infinity.\n\nThe conditional-expectation projection identity [[Durrett](#ref-Durrett), Theorem 4.1.15 and its proof] now yields $$v(F'_K)=K\\tau^2-\\mathbb E(U_K-T'_K)^2\\sim K\\tau^2.$$ In particular, this variance is positive for all sufficiently large $K$. The central limit theorem [[Durrett](#ref-Durrett), Theorem 3.4.1] gives $U_K/(\\sqrt K\\tau)\\Rightarrow N(0,1)$. By <a href=\"#eq:loss\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:loss\">[eq:loss]</a>, replacing $U_K$ by $T'_K$ changes the normalized variable by a term tending to zero in $L^2$. Moreover, $\\sqrt{v(F'_K)}/(\\sqrt K\\tau)\\to1$. The addition and rescaling forms of Slutsky’s theorem [[Durrett](#ref-Durrett), Exercises 3.2.13–3.2.14] therefore give $$\\frac{T'_K}{\\sqrt{v(F'_K)}}\\Rightarrow N(0,1).$$ These new standardized scores are centered and have variance one. Lemma <a href=\"#lem:mc-transfer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:mc-transfer\">10</a> restores all three inequalities in <a href=\"#eq:invariant\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:invariant\">[eq:invariant]</a> for sufficiently large $K$. At the same time, <a href=\"#eq:group\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:group\">[eq:group]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:degree\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:degree\">[eq:degree]</a> give $$\\frac{v(F'_K)}{D'_K}\\longrightarrow\n \\frac vD\\frac{V_Z}{c+\\delta}>\\lambda\\frac vD.$$ One sufficiently large finite $K$ therefore restores every maintained condition and increases the ratio by a factor greater than $\\lambda$.\n\nFor any prescribed $C>0$, choose a positive integer $k$ with $\\lambda^k>4C^2$. Perform $k$ such steps, choosing a finite batch size separately at each step. The final observation remains finite and satisfies $$v(F)/D>4C^2,\\qquad \\mathbb E|T_F|>\\tfrac12\\sqrt{v(F)}.$$ Let $N$ now denote the total number of bits underlying the final observation. Its sign readout $f=\\operatorname{sign}(T_F)$, with sign $1$ at zero, has degree at most $D$. Conditioning the bit sum on $F$ gives the signed identity $$\\sum_i\\widehat f(\\{i\\})=\\mathbb E[fH_N]=\\mathbb E|T_F|\n >\\tfrac12\\sqrt{v(F)}>C\\sqrt D\\ge C\\sqrt{\\deg(f)}.$$ The positive signed correlation excludes constant $f$. Arbitrarily large target ratios follow from the same finite iteration. ◻",
          "source_id": "PM-192.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-amplify-mean-cost-advantage",
      "inputs": [
        "Fixed partition with retained variance above mean cost",
        "Pointwise label cost bounds",
        "Finite group-label score moments",
        "Law of large numbers and central limit theorem"
      ],
      "method": "Retain controlled-cost label words, merge the remaining words, bound variance loss and restore the normalized-score invariant before each iteration.",
      "output": "Arbitrarily large retained variance-to-degree ratios and a final Boolean sign readout without additional averaging.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-mc-transfer-2"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-weighted-event",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-weighted-event",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-weighted-event</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Exactly one bin contains $g_0$. The first term in <a href=\"#app:weighted-cell\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"app:weighted-cell\">[app:weighted-cell]</a> tests all unselected leaves; subtracting the all-leaves-pass indicator requires the selected leaf to fail. This proves the identity pointwise, including all endpoints. The boundary assertion follows from the finite collections of bin and leaf-interval endpoints. It remains to prove the variance saving. We let $R\\to\\infty$ for this calculation and fix a finite value only after the estimates.\n\n\n###### Conditional moments.\n\n\nWrite $\\phi$ for the standard normal density. Symmetry of the two tails gives $v_m=0$; for $i<m$, the conditional mean lies in the closure of $B_i$, so $|v_i|\\le R$ and $u_i\\le\\Delta^2$. Integration by parts gives $$\\int_R^\\infty t^2\\phi(t)\\,dt\n =R\\phi(R)+\\int_R^\\infty\\phi(t)\\,dt,\n \\qquad\n \\int_R^\\infty\\phi(t)\\,dt\\le\\frac{\\phi(R)}R.$$ Thus $$\\label{app:weighted-bin-moments}\n \\pi_m u_m=O(Re^{-R^2/2}),\\qquad\n \\sum_i\\pi_i v_i=0,\\qquad\n \\sum_i\\pi_i v_i^2=1-\\sum_i\\pi_i u_i=1-o(1),$$ where the last equality follows from $\\sum_i\\pi_i u_i\\le\\Delta^2+O(Re^{-R^2/2})=o(1)$.\n\n\nFor the leaf intervals, write $$\\alpha_j=\\mathbb P(G_j\\in I_j),\\qquad\n t_j=\\mathbb E[G_j\\mid G_j\\in I_j],\\qquad\n r_j=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(G_j\\mid G_j\\in I_j).$$ The bounded normal density and the interval lengths imply, uniformly in $j$ for sufficiently large $R$, $$\\label{app:weighted-rare-moments}\n 0<\\alpha_j\\le Ch<\\tfrac12,\\qquad\n |t_j-(v_j+1)|\\le h/2,\\qquad 0\\le r_j\\le h^2.$$ Constants in this section are absolute unless stated otherwise. Let $d_j,b_j$ be the conditional mean and variance of $G_j$ on $I_j^c$. Using the unconditional mean zero and variance one gives $$\\label{app:weighted-complement}\n d_j=-\\frac{\\alpha_jt_j}{1-\\alpha_j},\\qquad\n b_j=\\frac{1-\\alpha_jr_j}{1-\\alpha_j}\n       -\\frac{\\alpha_jt_j^2}{(1-\\alpha_j)^2}.$$ Indeed the complementary second moment is $(1-\\alpha_j(r_j+t_j^2))/(1-\\alpha_j)$, from which we subtract $d_j^2$.\n\n\n###### The mixture identity.\n\n\n\nThe event $A$ changes the distribution of the selected bin. Put $$w_i=\\pi_i\\frac{1-\\alpha_i}{\\alpha_i},\\qquad\n W=\\sum_iw_i,\\qquad \\omega_i=\\frac{w_i}{W}.$$ Independence gives $$\\label{app:weighted-probability}\n p_A=W\\prod_{j=1}^m\\alpha_j>0,\n \\qquad \\mathbb P(G_0\\in B_i\\mid G\\in A)=\\omega_i.$$ Here $W$ is finite and positive for every finite $R$. Within $A\\cap\\{G_0\\in B_i\\}$ the coordinates remain independent under their respective bin, interval, and complementary-interval restrictions. The conditional mean and variance of $L$ are therefore $$\\sum_{j=1}^mt_j-1+e_i,\n \\qquad u_i+\\sum_jr_j+b_i-r_i,\n \\qquad e_i=v_i+1-\\frac{t_i}{1-\\alpha_i}.$$ The small mean error satisfies $$\\label{app:weighted-mean-error}\n |e_i|\\le h/2+\\frac{\\alpha_i|t_i|}{1-\\alpha_i}\n          =O(h(R+2)).$$ Applying the variance decomposition with the conditional weights $\\omega_i$, not the original weights $\\pi_i$, gives the exact identity $$\\begin{aligned}\n W\\bigl(1-\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(L\\mid G\\in A)\\bigr)\n &=\\sum_iw_i(1-b_i+r_i)-\\sum_iw_iu_i\\notag\\\\\n &\\quad-W\\sum_jr_j-W\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}_{i\\sim\\omega}(e_i).\n \\label{app:weighted-variance-identity}\n\\end{aligned}$$ We next show that the first sum has lower limit at least one and that each of the three subtracted terms tends to zero. This will give the strict conditional variance bound despite the possibly large total weight $W$.\n\nThe schedule in <a href=\"#app:weighted-schedule\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"app:weighted-schedule\">[app:weighted-schedule]</a> makes the bin scale $\\Delta^2/h$, the aggregate leaf scale $mh$, and the tail scale $e^{-R^2/2}/h$ tend to zero even after multiplication by $e^{3R}$ and any fixed power of $R$. The leaf intervals are narrow enough to control their total variance, but wide enough that inverse interval probabilities do not overwhelm the bin and tail estimates below.\n\n\n###### The positive contribution.\n\n\nSubstituting <a href=\"#app:weighted-complement\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"app:weighted-complement\">[app:weighted-complement]</a> yields $$\\label{app:weighted-positive-term}\n w_i(1-b_i+r_i)\n =\\pi_i\\left(-1+\\frac{r_i}{\\alpha_i}\n                    +\\frac{t_i^2}{1-\\alpha_i}\\right)\n \\ge\\pi_i(t_i^2-1).$$ Since $t_i=v_i+1+O(h)$ uniformly, we have $$\\sum_i\\pi_i(t_i^2-1)\n =\\sum_i\\pi_i v_i^2+2\\sum_i\\pi_i v_i+O(h(R+2))\n =1+o(1)$$ by <a href=\"#app:weighted-bin-moments\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"app:weighted-bin-moments\">[app:weighted-bin-moments]</a>. The shift of the leaf intervals by one is important: it produces the term $t_i^2-1$, whose average tends to one.\n\n\n###### The three error terms.\n\nFor $i<m$, the fact that $v_i$ belongs to the closure of $B_i$ gives the density-ratio bound $$\\begin{aligned}\n w_i\\le\\frac{\\pi_i}{\\alpha_i}\n &\\le\\frac{\\Delta}{h}\n       \\frac{\\phi(\\max\\{0,|v_i|-\\Delta\\})}\n            {\\phi(|v_i|+1+h)}\\notag\\\\\n &\\le\\frac{\\Delta}{h}e^{3R}\n \\label{app:weighted-density-ratio}\n\\end{aligned}$$ for all sufficiently large $R$. For the final inequality, put $x=|v_i|\\le R$. The logarithm of the density ratio is at most $$\\frac{(x+1+h)^2-\\max\\{0,x-\\Delta\\}^2}{2}\n \\le(1+h+\\Delta)x+\\frac{(1+h)^2}{2}\\le3R.$$ The middle inequality follows by expanding when $x\\ge\\Delta$; when $x<\\Delta$, use $x^2/2\\le\\Delta x$. For the tail bin, $v_m=0$, so $\\alpha_m\\ge h\\phi(1+h)$ and $w_m\\le C\\pi_m/h$. Summing and using $(m-1)\\Delta=2R$ gives $$\\label{app:weighted-total-weight}\n W\\le\\frac{2Re^{3R}+C}{h}.$$\n\nFirst, $u_i\\le\\Delta^2$ on interior bins and $\\pi_mu_m=O(Re^{-R^2/2})$ on the tail bin. Since $\\Delta^2\\le4R^2e^{-R^2/2}$, we obtain $$\\sum_iw_iu_i\n \\le W\\Delta^2+C\\pi_mu_m/h\n =O(R^3e^{3R-R^2/8})+O(Re^{-R^2/8})=o(1).$$ Second, $\\sum_jr_j\\le mh^2$ and $mh=O(e^{-R^2/8})$, so $$W\\sum_jr_j\\le Wmh^2\n =O((Re^{3R}+1)e^{-R^2/8})=o(1).$$ Third, <a href=\"#app:weighted-mean-error\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"app:weighted-mean-error\">[app:weighted-mean-error]</a> gives $$W\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}_{i\\sim\\omega}(e_i)\n \\le W\\,O(h^2(R+2)^2)\n =O((Re^{3R}+1)(R+2)^2e^{-3R^2/8})=o(1).$$ Each negative quadratic exponent dominates the linear exponent and polynomial factors. <a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-exceptional-cell-cancellation**: The exceptional report identifies exactly the event that the selected leaf alone fails its interval test. Its indicator equals the cancellation expression pointwise.
- **invariant-report-cell-degree**: Every attained report cell has indicator degree at most the reporting cost, including the exceptional cell whose degree saving comes from cancellation.
- **invariant-retained-variance-bound**: For independent centered variance-one inputs, retained variance is at least the reporting cost plus the exceptional-event predictor deficit.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-finite-check-as-universal-refutation**: Treat a finite reporting-rule check as certification of the arbitrarily large Boolean counterexamples. -> The finite check establishes reporting semantics and the retained-variance inequality for its supplied inputs; it does not establish a strict Gaussian gain or the subsequent amplification limits.
- **anti-use-coordinate-cost-as-query-count**: Interpret the cell degree bound as the number of coordinates that must be queried to compute the report. -> The exceptional event may depend on all inputs even though each summand of its indicator expansion uses fewer coordinates.
- **anti-use-dependent-inputs**: Apply the retained-variance calculation to correlated inputs merely because their individual variances agree. -> The omitted coordinate need not remain centered with unchanged second moment after conditioning on the reported coordinates.
- **anti-use-stage-uniformity**: Read finite verification or a fixed-observation moment bound as a uniform bound for every amplification stage. -> The observation and required copy count can change between stages, so a bound for one averaging limit does not supply a global resource estimate.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. This harness checks only the finite refined-report construction and inequality of Lemma 5 on independent uniform signs. It exhaustively constructs every report cell, verifies the exceptional indicator identity, checks the cell degree bound, and computes retained variance exactly. It does not certify Theorem 1: the source chooses Gaussian parameters and averaging counts through sufficiently large finite limits and supplies no useful growth bounds.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON with id, closed intervals, selector indices for negative and positive signs, and rational b.
# PRECONDITIONS: At least three leaf intervals; independent uniform signs; valid indices and ordered rational endpoints.
# OUTPUTS: Ordered reports, exceptional indicator expansion values, retained variance, and predictor deficit as exact rationals.
# TIME: Exhaustive enumeration of the finite sign cube and aggregation of its report cells.
# SPACE: Stores the enumerated reports, indicator values, and cell aggregates.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact integer and rational arithmetic.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception and produces no accepted certificate.
import itertools
import json
import sys
from fractions import Fraction


def rational(value):
    if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, (int, str)):
        raise ValueError('rational values must be integers or strings')
    return Fraction(value)


def run(data):
    intervals = data['intervals']
    m = len(intervals)
    if m < 3:
        raise ValueError('too few leaves')
    bounds = []
    for interval in intervals:
        if not isinstance(interval, list) or len(interval) != 2:
            raise ValueError('invalid interval')
        lo, hi = map(rational, interval)
        if lo > hi:
            raise ValueError('unordered endpoints')
        bounds.append((lo, hi))
    selector = data['selector']
    if not isinstance(selector, list) or len(selector) != 2:
        raise ValueError('invalid selector')
    if any(type(i) is not int or not 0 <= i < m for i in selector):
        raise ValueError('invalid selected index')
    b = rational(data['b'])
    reports = []
    indicator_values = []
    cells = {}
    deficit_total = Fraction(0)
    for row in itertools.product((-1, 1), repeat=m + 1):
        u, z = row[0], row[1:]
        i = selector[0 if u < 0 else 1]
        passes = [lo <= value <= hi for value, (lo, hi) in zip(z, bounds)]
        in_B = all(passes)
        in_P = all(passes[j] for j in range(m) if j != i)
        in_A = in_P and not in_B
        expansion = sum(int(i == k) * int(all(passes[j] for j in range(m) if j != k)) for k in range(m)) - int(in_B)
        indicator_values.append(expansion)
        if in_A:
            report = ('A',)
        elif in_B:
            report = ('B', tuple(z))
        else:
            report = ('Pc', u, i, tuple(z[j] for j in range(m) if j != i))
        reports.append(report)
        count, total = cells.get(report, (0, 0))
        L = sum(row)
        cells[report] = (count + 1, total + L)
        if in_A:
            deficit_total += 1 - (Fraction(L) - b) ** 2
    population = len(reports)
    retained = sum((Fraction(total * total, count * population) for count, total in cells.values()), Fraction(0))
    deficit = deficit_total / population
    return {'reports': reports, 'indicator_values': indicator_values, 'retained_variance': str(retained), 'predictor_deficit': str(deficit)}


if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(json.dumps(run(json.load(sys.stdin)), separators=(',', ':')))
```

    "oracle_code": "import itertools\nimport json\nimport math\nimport sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\n\ndef rational(value):\n    if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, (int, data['intervals']\n    m = len(raw_intervals)\n    if m < 3:\n        return False\n    intervals = []\n    for item in raw_intervals:\n        if not isinstance(item, list) or len(item) != 2:\n            return False\n        lo, hi = rational(item[0]), rational(item[1])\n        if lo > hi:\n            return False\n        intervals.append((lo, hi))\n    selector = data['selector']\n    if not len(reports) != population or len(values) != population:\n        return False\n    cell_rows = {}\n    exceptional_rows = []\n    for position, row in enumerate(rows):\n        selected = selector[int(row[0] > 0)]\n        failed = [j for j in range(m) if not intervals[j][0] <= row[j + 1] <= intervals[j][1]]\n        if failed == [selected]:\n            correct_report = ['A']\n            exceptional_rows.append(row)\n            correct_indicator = 1\n        elif len(failed) == 0:\n            correct_report = ['B', list(row[1:])]\n            correct_indicator = 0\n        else:\n            remaining = list(row[1:])\n            del remaining[selected]\n            correct_report = ['Pc', row[0], selected, remaining]\n            correct_indicator = 0\n        if reports[position] != correct_report:\n            return False\n        if type(values[position]) is not int or values[position] != correct_indicator:\n            return False\n        key = json.dumps(reports[position], separators=(',', ':'))\n        cell_rows.setdefault(key, []).append(row)\n    # With m+1 sign coordinates the only Fourier coefficient above degree m\n    # is the coefficient of the product of all coordinates.\n    for members in cell_rows.values():\n        if sum(math.prod(row) for row in members) != 0:\n            return False\n    variance = Fraction(0)\n    for members in cell_rows.values():\n        conditional_mean = Fraction(sum(sum(row) for row in members), len(members))\n        variance += Fraction(len(members), population) * conditional_mean ** 2\n    delta = sum((1 - (Fraction(sum(row)) - b) ** 2 for row in exceptional_rows), Fraction(0)) / population\n    if rational(candidate['retained_variance']) != variance:\n        return False\n    if rational(candidate['predictor_deficit']) != delta:\n        return False\n    if variance < m + delta:\n        return False\n    return True\n\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    try:\n        with open(sys.argv[1], encoding='utf-8') as handle:\n            data = json.load(handle)\n        with open(sys.argv[2], encoding='utf-8') as handle:\n            candidate = json.load(handle)\n        accepted = verify(data, candidate, sys.argv[3])\n    except Exception:\n        accepted = False\n    sys.exit(0 if accepted else 1)\n",
    "output_contract": "JSON contains reports and indicator_values in lexicographic sign-cube order, retained_variance, and predictor_deficit. Reports are ['A'], ['B', leaf_values], or ['Pc', central_value, selected_index, unselected_leaf_values]. Rational quantities use exact Fraction string syntax. The oracle checks every report, the cancellation values, every cell's degree bound, exact retained variance and deficit, and the retained-variance inequality.",
    "reason": "This harness checks only the finite refined-report construction and inequality of Lemma 5 on independent uniform signs. It exhaustively constructs every report cell, verifies the exceptional indicator identity, checks the cell degree bound, and computes retained variance exactly. It does not certify Theorem 1: the source chooses Gaussian parameters and averaging counts through sufficiently large finite limits and supplies no useful growth bounds.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON with id, closed intervals, selector indices for negative and positive signs, and rational b.\n# PRECONDITIONS: At least three leaf intervals; independent uniform signs; valid indices and ordered rational endpoints.\n# OUTPUTS: Ordered reports, exceptional indicator expansion values, retained variance, and predictor deficit as exact rationals.\n# TIME: Exhaustive enumeration of the finite sign cube and aggregation of its report cells.\n# SPACE: Stores the enumerated reports, indicator values, and cell aggregates.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact integer and rational arithmetic.\n# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception and produces no accepted certificate.\nimport itertools\nimport json\nimport sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\n\ndef rational(value):\n    if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, (int, str)):\n        raise ValueError('rational values must be integers or strings')\n    return Fraction(value)\n\n\ndef run(data):\n    intervals = data['intervals']\n    m = len(intervals)\n    if m < 3:\n        raise ValueError('too few leaves')\n    bounds = []\n    for interval in intervals:\n        if not isinstance(interval, list) or len(interval) != 2:\n            raise ValueError('invalid interval')\n        lo, hi = map(rational, interval)\n        if lo > hi:\n            raise ValueError('unordered endpoints')\n        bounds.append((lo, hi))\n    selector = data['selector']\n    if not isinstance(selector, list) or len(selector) != 2:\n        raise ValueError('invalid selector')\n    if any(type(i) is not int or not 0 <= i < m for i in selector):\n        raise ValueError('invalid selected index')\n    b = rational(data['b'])\n    reports = []\n    indicator_values = []\n    cells = {}\n    deficit_total = Fraction(0)\n    for row in itertools.product((-1, 1), repeat=m + 1):\n        u, z = row[0], row[1:]\n        i = selector[0 if u < 0 else 1]\n        passes = [lo <= value <= hi for value, (lo, hi) in zip(z, bounds)]\n        in_B = all(passes)\n        in_P = all(passes[j] for j in range(m) if j != i)\n        in_A = in_P and not in_B\n        expansion = sum(int(i == k) * int(all(passes[j] for j in range(m) if j != k)) for k in range(m)) - int(in_B)\n        indicator_values.append(expansion)\n        if in_A:\n            report = ('A',)\n        elif in_B:\n            report = ('B', tuple(z))\n        else:\n            report = ('Pc', u, i, tuple(z[j] for j in range(m) if j != i))\n        reports.append(report)\n        count, total = cells.get(report, (0, 0))\n        L = sum(row)\n        cells[report] = (count + 1, total + L)\n        if in_A:\n            deficit_total += 1 - (Fraction(L) - b) ** 2\n    population = len(reports)\n    retained = sum((Fraction(total * total, count * population) for count, total in cells.values()), Fraction(0))\n    deficit = deficit_total / population\n    return {'reports': reports, 'indicator_values': indicator_values, 'retained_variance': str(retained), 'predictor_deficit': str(deficit)}\n\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    print(json.dumps(run(json.load(sys.stdin)), separators=(',', ':')))\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The amplification proof chooses sufficiently large finite copy counts separately for each observation. It supplies no resource contract for a practical software implementation of the resulting Boolean counterexamples. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The Gaussian interval construction establishes a prediction-error saving for independent standard normal coordinates. It provides no game dynamics, simulation update rule, or approximation guarantee for a simulated physical process. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The transfer lemma is a conditional convergence result requiring Gaussian-null discontinuities and moment control. It does not provide an automated procedure for checking these preconditions or choosing a finite approximation size. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | For supplied intervals, selector, endpoint conventions, and finite input alphabets, the refined reporting rule is a concrete finite transformation. Its output indicators have the stated coordinate decomposition; a positive variance gain additionally requires the probabilistic conditions and parameter choices in the paper. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Merging output words gives an everywhere-valid polynomial degree bound for cell indicators. That bound does not specify a compact representation, indexing scheme, or storage bound for the retained words. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The Gaussian parameter schedule controls asymptotic prediction errors and rare-event probabilities. It supplies no precision, throughput, parallel execution, or hardware architecture contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main result disproves a universal relation between singleton Fourier sums and real multilinear degree. It establishes no cryptographic primitive, adversarial model, or security guarantee for the constructed functions. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Taking the sign of an observation score produces a Boolean counterexample with large correlation. The readout lemma supplies neither a learning procedure from samples nor a generalization or sample-complexity guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The coarse-report result compares a specified predictor with the squared-error optimum given its report. This conditional-expectation inequality is not an optimisation algorithm for selecting intervals, reports, or a partition. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The switching construction adds an independent score and specifies what information an observation reveals. Independence here is probabilistic; the construction gives no communication, synchronization, or fault-tolerance protocol. |
| other (Analysis of Boolean functions and finite observation partitions) | True | strong | adaptable | stated | The complement-refinement construction preserves a saving event as a cell and intersects its complement with a finite rectangular grid. Under the stated boundary, conditional-variance, and coordinate-decomposition hypotheses, this produces a partition whose retained variance exceeds its average indicator degree cost. |

          "anchor": "eq:indicator",
          "exact_quote": "Let $m\\ge3$, let $I_1,\\ldots,I_m$ be intervals, and let $\\iota:\\mathbb R\\to\\{1,\\ldots,m\\}$ be a selector with finitely many cut points. All endpoint and tie conventions are part of the rule. Write its inputs as $(u,z_1,\\ldots,z_m)$ and their sum as $L=u+\\sum_{j=1}^m z_j$. The coordinate $u$ selects the leaf $z_{\\iota(u)}$. Define $$B=\\{z_j\\in I_j\\text{ for every }j\\},\\qquad\n P=\\{z_j\\in I_j\\text{ for every }j\\ne\\iota(u)\\},\\qquad\n A=P\\setminus B.$$ Thus $A$ is the event that the selected leaf alone fails its interval test. Since $B\\subseteq P$, the sets $A$, $B$, and $P^c$ partition the input space. Define the tagged observation $\\mathcal W$ by $$\\begin{array}{c|l}\n \\text{Event}&\\text{Information reported}\\\\ \\hline\n A&\\text{the tag }A\\text{ only}\\\\\n B&(B,z_1,\\ldots,z_m)\\\\\n P^c&(P^c,u,\\iota(u),(z_j)_{j\\ne\\iota(u)}).\n\\end{array}$$ On $B$, the central input is omitted. On $P^c$, the selected leaf is omitted. Although the exceptional report on $A$ can depend on all $m+1$ inputs, its indicator has the pointwise identity $$\\label{eq:indicator}\n \\mathbf1_A=\n \\sum_{i=1}^m\\mathbf1_{\\{\\iota(u)=i\\}}\n                  \\prod_{j\\ne i}\\mathbf1_{I_j}(z_j)\n -\\prod_{j=1}^m\\mathbf1_{I_j}(z_j).$$ Each summand uses only $m$ coordinates. This cancellation is what allows the exceptional report to have the same degree cost as the ordinary reports.",

          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-report",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-report</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Identity <a href=\"#eq:indicator\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:indicator\">[eq:indicator]</a> gives the required decomposition for the $A$ report. An attained $B$ report fixes the $m$ leaf values and imposes no condition on $u$. An attained $P^c$ report fixes $u$, its selected index $i$, and the $m-1$ unselected leaves. At least one of these reported leaves fails its test; hence the reported values already force $P^c$, whatever the omitted value $z_i$ may be. Its indicator therefore depends on only the $m$ reported coordinates. Unused output labels have zero indicators. This proves the first assertion on every input, including interval endpoints.\n\nTo estimate the retained variance, predict $L$ by $b$ on $A$, by $\\sum_jz_j$ on $B$, and by $u+\\sum_{j\\ne\\iota(u)}z_j$ on $P^c$. This predictor is a function of the reported information. On $B$ its error is $u$; independence gives $\\mathbb E[\\mathbf1_Bu^2]=\\mathbb P(B)$. For the last type of report, fix an index $i$ and let $E_i=P^c\\cap\\{\\iota(u)=i\\}$. This event and its reported data depend only on $u$ and the leaves other than $z_i$. Consequently $$\\mathbb E[\\mathbf1_{E_i}z_i]=0,\n \\qquad\n \\mathbb E[\\mathbf1_{E_i}z_i^2]=\\mathbb P(E_i).$$ Summing the squared errors over the three types of report gives $$\\mathbb P(A^c)+\\mathbb E[\\mathbf1_A(L-b)^2]=1-\\Delta_b.$$ Conditional expectation has no larger mean squared error [[Durrett](#ref-Durrett), Theorems 4.1.13 and 4.1.15]. Since $L$ is centered and $\\operatorname{Var}(L)=m+1$, the conditional-variance identity gives <a href=\"#eq:retained\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:retained\">[eq:retained]</a>. ◻",

          "anchor": "app:small-width-estimates",
          "exact_quote": "#### Gaussian estimates, including the rare-probability factors\n\nIn this subsection $U=G$ is standard normal and $w\\downarrow0$. Write $\\phi$ for its density. Uniformly over the grid, $$q_j\\le\\frac{w}{\\sqrt{2\\pi}},\\qquad\n |\\mu_j-(t_j+1)|\\le w/2,\\qquad\n \\sum_ja_j\\le mw^2=O(Tw^{4/3})\\longrightarrow0.$$ We must control the two errors in <a href=\"#app:small-width-deficit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"app:small-width-deficit\">[app:small-width-deficit]</a> after division by the selected interval probability. A uniform lower bound of order $w$ for all $q_j$ is unavailable, because the grid extends into the Gaussian tails. Instead we compare each interval’s density with the density of the central coordinate that selects it.\n\nFor $|t|\\le T$ and $z\\in A_{I(t)}$, we have $|z-t|\\le1+h+w/2$. Hence, for sufficiently small $w$, $$q_{I(t)}\\ge w\\inf_{z\\in A_{I(t)}}\\phi(z),\\qquad\n \\frac{\\phi(t)}{q_{I(t)}}\n \\le w^{-1}\\exp(C(1+T)).$$ The constant $C$ is absolute: the logarithm of the density ratio is $(z^2-t^2)/2\\le |t||z-t|+|z-t|^2/2$. Since $T=2\\sqrt{\\log(1/w)}$, for each fixed $\\varepsilon>0$, $$\\label{app:small-width-inverse-density}\n \\frac{\\phi(t)}{q_{I(t)}}=O(w^{-1-\\varepsilon})\n \\qquad (|t|\\le T).$$ The fallback interval is centered at $1$, so $q_{i_*}\\ge cw$ for an absolute $c>0$ and small $w$. Integrating the central bound and using this fallback bound on the tails gives $$\\label{app:small-width-inverse-mean}\n \\mathbb E\\frac1{q_{I(G)}}=O(Tw^{-1-\\varepsilon}).$$ Take $\\varepsilon=1/6$. Since $0\\le S_a-a_{I(G)}\\le mw^2$, the variance contribution in <a href=\"#app:small-width-deficit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"app:small-width-deficit\">[app:small-width-deficit]</a> is at most $$mw^2\\mathbb E\\frac1{q_{I(G)}}\n =O(T^2w^{1/6})\\longrightarrow0.$$\n\nFor the squared mismatch, first suppose $|t|\\le T$. Then $$|t+1-\\mu_{I(t)}|\n \\le |t-t_{I(t)}|+w/2\\le h+w/2.$$ Its central contribution is therefore at most $$O\\bigl(Tw^{-7/6}(h+w)^2\\bigr)\n =O(Tw^{1/6})\\longrightarrow0.$$ On $|t|>T$, the fallback mean is bounded, so the contribution is $$O\\left(w^{-1}\\int_{|t|>T}(1+t^2)\\phi(t)\\,dt\\right)\n =O\\bigl(w^{-1}(1+T)e^{-T^2/2}\\bigr)\n =O((1+T)w)\\longrightarrow0.$$ For the integral estimate, integration by parts gives $\\int_T^\\infty t^2\\phi(t)\\,dt\n=T\\phi(T)+\\int_T^\\infty\\phi(t)\\,dt$, and $\\int_T^\\infty\\phi(t)\\,dt\\le\\phi(T)/T$ for $T>0$. The last equality uses $e^{-T^2/2}=w^2$. Both subtracted errors therefore vanish, proving $$\\label{app:small-width-limit}\n H_w(G)\\longrightarrow1.$$ Thus the same coarse report has a strictly positive Gaussian improvement for this independent choice of parameters. To use it repeatedly on finite cubes, we now fix those parameters and transfer the particular lower bound just proved.",
          "source_object": "Gaussian estimates with rare-probability factors"
        }

          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-readout</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Fix $C>0$ and choose $F$ with $v(F)>4C^2D$. For a sufficiently large finite $M$, the displayed sum exceeds $C\\sqrt{MD}\\ge C\\sqrt{\\deg(f)}$. It is positive, so $f$ is nonconstant. ◻",

          "exact_quote": "#### Switching to an independent score\n\nKeep the fixed Gaussian event $A$ and predictor $b$ above, and put $q=m+1$. Add one independent centered variance-one input $w$ to the original tuple $\\mathbf z=(u,z_1,\\ldots,z_m)$. Define $$\\mathcal O_+(\\mathbf z,w)=\n \\begin{cases}\n   (\\mathrm{off},\\mathbf z),&\\mathbf z\\notin A,\\\\\n   (\\mathrm{on},w),&\\mathbf z\\in A.\n \\end{cases}$$ Off $A$ the original sum $L$ is known and $w$ is omitted. On $A$ the extra input is known and the original sum must be predicted. The rule gains variance whenever the conditional variance of $L$ on $A$ is smaller than the one unit of residual variance of $w$.\n\nThis rule has $q+1$ inputs but cell cost at most $q$. Indeed, an off-$A$ output fixes the first $q$ inputs and places no condition on $w$. The indicator of an on-$A$ output $(\\mathrm{on},w_0)$ is $$\\mathbf 1_A(\\mathbf z)\\mathbf 1_{\\{w=w_0\\}}.$$ Multiplying <a href=\"#eq:indicator\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:indicator\">[eq:indicator]</a> by the last value test expresses this indicator as a sum of functions of at most $m+1=q$ inputs. The tags keep the two types of report separate, and unattained labels have zero indicators. These pointwise expansions also cover the prescribed endpoints and ties. The rule has finite range on every finite product alphabet.\n\n\n\nTo establish a gain for the approximating laws, first transfer the unconditional deficit, before conditioning on $A$. For independent copies of any centered variance-one law $Z_n$ tending to a standard normal with uniformly bounded fourth moments, Lemma <a href=\"#lem:transfer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:transfer\">2</a> gives $$\\Delta_{b,n}:=\\mathbb E[\\mathbf 1_A(1-(L-b)^2)]\n \\longrightarrow d_A>\\frac34p_*.$$ Thus, for all sufficiently large $n$, $\\Delta_{b,n}>\\eta=p_*/2$. Since $\\Delta_{b,n}\\le\\mathbb P(A)$, these laws have positive probability of $A$. Only now fix such an $n$, write $\\Delta_b=\\Delta_{b,n}$, and define $$p=\\mathbb P(A)>0,\\qquad c=\\mathbb E[L\\mid A].$$ Independence of $w$ from the original tuple shows that $\\mathbb E[L+w\\mid\\mathcal O_+]$ equals $L$ off $A$ and $c+w$ on $A$. The sum $L+w$ is centered, so its conditional mean is centered too. Using $\\mathbb Ew=0$, $\\mathbb Ew^2=1$, and $\\mathbb EL^2=q$ gives the exact identity $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(\\mathbb E[L+w\\mid\\mathcal O_+])\n &=\\mathbb E[\\mathbf 1_{A^c}L^2]+p(c^2+1)\\notag\\\\\n &=q+p\\bigl(1-\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(L\\mid A)\\bigr).\n \\label{eq:switching-variance}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The same input law satisfies $$\\label{eq:switching-correction}\n p\\bigl(1-\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(L\\mid A)\\bigr)\n =\\Delta_b+p(c-b)^2>\\eta.$$ The identities in <a href=\"#eq:switching-variance\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:switching-variance\">[eq:switching-variance]</a> and the equality in <a href=\"#eq:switching-correction\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:switching-correction\">[eq:switching-correction]</a> hold more generally for any independent centered variance-one original inputs and an independent centered variance-one $w$, whenever $p>0$, with $\\Delta_b=\\mathbb E[\\mathbf 1_A(1-(L-b)^2)]$. Their marginal laws need not agree. The nonnegative correction $p(c-b)^2$ records the improvement from the fixed Gaussian predictor $b$ to the actual conditional mean $c$. For the fixed approximating law, <a href=\"#eq:switching-variance\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:switching-variance\">[eq:switching-variance]</a> therefore exceeds $q+\\eta$. This argument uses convergence of one fixed predictor error, not continuity of conditional variances.\n\nThe pointwise cost and the transferred gain verify Proposition <a href=\"#prop:amplification\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:amplification\">4</a> with the distinct triple $$(r,d,\\eta)=(m+2,m+1,p_*/2).$$ The criterion again proves Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>, with ratio multiplier at least $1+\\eta/(m+1)>1$. For both rules in this section, the Gaussian parameters and $\\eta$ stay fixed throughout. Each stage starts from one fixed finite observation and chooses its own finite averaging count; the fourth-moment bound is uniform in that count, not across all stages.",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-refinement",
          "exact_quote": "Let $q\\ge2$, let $G=(G_1,\\ldots,G_q)$ have independent standard normal coordinates, and put $S_G=\\sum_iG_i$. Suppose a measurable set $A\\subset\\mathbb R^q$ has Gaussian-null boundary, $$p_A:=\\mathbb P(G\\in A)>0,\\qquad\n r_A:=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(S_G\\mid G\\in A)<1,$$ and its indicator, on every product of finite alphabets, is a finite linear combination of functions each depending on at most $q-1$ coordinates. Then there is a finite pointwise partition $\\mathcal P$ with one cell $A$ of cost $q-1$ and all other cells of cost $q$ that has Gaussian-null cell boundaries and the prescribed pointwise coordinate bounds. Writing $$V=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(\\mathbb E[S_G\\mid\\mathcal P(G)]),\\qquad\n c=\\mathbb E\\ell(\\mathcal P(G)),$$ we have $V>c=q-p_A$.",
          "source_object": "Finite complement-refinement partition"

          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-refinement",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-refinement</summary>\n\n*Proof.* Partition $[-M,M)$ into finitely many half-open intervals of length at most $h$, and add the two tail intervals. Approximate a real coordinate by the midpoint of its bounded interval and by zero on the tails. For a standard normal coordinate, the expected squared error is at most $$h^2+\\mathbb E[G_1^2\\mathbf 1_{\\{|G_1|\\ge M\\}}].$$ Let $\\widetilde S_G$ be the sum of these coordinate approximations. It is constant on each box of the resulting finite rectangular grid. Since $(\\sum_i a_i)^2\\le q\\sum_i a_i^2$, choosing finite $M$ large enough and then $h>0$ small enough ensures $$\\mathbb E(S_G-\\widetilde S_G)^2<p_A(1-r_A).$$\n\nGive $A$ one label and give every intersection of $A^c$ with a grid box its own label. Retain this partition pointwise, even on cells of zero Gaussian probability. Every new cell has boundary contained in the boundary of $A$ together with the finitely many grid hyperplanes, and hence has Gaussian-null boundary. Give $A$ cost $q-1$ and every other cell cost $q$. The special-cell algebraic requirement is assumed; the others are automatic for functions of $q$ inputs. Conditional means minimize squared error on every cell [[Durrett](#ref-Durrett), Theorem 4.1.15], so $$\\mathbb E\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(S_G\\mid\\mathcal P(G))\n \\le p_A r_A+\n       \\mathbb E[\\mathbf 1_{A^c}(S_G-\\widetilde S_G)^2]<p_A.$$ Since $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(S_G)=q$, it follows that $V>q-p_A=\\mathbb E\\ell(\\mathcal P(G))=c$. ◻",
          "source_object": "Rectangular-grid refinement construction"

          "anchor": "stmt-thm-mean-cost",

Suppose that, on every product of finite input alphabets, each cell indicator for $l$ is a finite linear combination of functions of at most $\\ell(l)$ input coordinates. Suppose also that every cell has boundary of standard Gaussian measure zero in $\\mathbb R^q$. For independent standard normals $G_1,\\ldots,G_q$, put $$S_G=\\sum_{i=1}^qG_i,\\qquad\n V=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(\\mathbb E[S_G\\mid\\mathcal P(G)]),\\qquad\n c=\\mathbb E\\ell(\\mathcal P(G)).$$ If $V>c$, then finite observations on sign cubes have arbitrarily large ratios $v(F)/D$, where $D>0$ is a cell degree bound. The Boolean conclusion of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a> follows by taking the sign of the final score, without a further averaging step. All partitions and observations are defined pointwise, including cells of zero Gaussian probability.",

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-real-degree-and-uniform-sign-measure** (formal_scope): The counterexamples concern Boolean functions under uniform sign measure and the degree of their real multilinear representation. They do not establish the corresponding claim for another degree notion or input measure. Blocked use: ['Transfer the refutation to finite-field degree, threshold degree, or a nonuniform input measure without a separate argument.']
- **limit-majority-and-square-root-distinction** (counterexample_scope): A violation of the majority benchmark need not violate the square-root bound. The main theorem establishes the stronger unbounded-factor refutation separately. Blocked use: ['Treat a counterexample to the majority benchmark alone as a counterexample to every constant multiple of the square-root degree bound.']
- **limit-subcube-partitions** (counterexample_scope): The construction uses polynomial-indicator cancellation beyond subcube observations. Its refutation does not remove the separate square-root guarantees for subcube or parity decision-tree settings. Blocked use: ['Infer failure of decision-tree square-root guarantees merely from the failure of the unrestricted polynomial-degree conjecture.']
- **limit-positive-score-normalization** (hypothesis): Standardized scores require strictly positive retained variance, and amplification uses a positive cell degree bound. A constant observation cannot supply the normalized score input required by the construction. Blocked use: ['Normalize a zero-variance observation or invoke amplification with a nonpositive degree bound.']
- **limit-fixed-predictor-transfer-hypotheses** (hypothesis): Transfer of quadratic predictor errors requires independent copies of centered variance-one approximating laws, Gaussian-null discontinuities, controlled growth, and uniformly bounded fourth moments. Blocked use: ['Transfer an unbounded predictor error from weak convergence alone.', 'Apply the predictor-error lemma to correlated copies or discontinuities of positive Gaussian measure.']
- **limit-pointwise-coordinate-decomposition** (hypothesis): The degree saving is a pointwise decomposition of every output indicator on every finite product alphabet. Dependence on fewer coordinates in each summand does not imply that the output itself ignores a fixed coordinate. Blocked use: ['Replace the algebraic decomposition requirement with an average or almost-sure coordinate bound.', 'Treat indicator degree cost as a bound on coordinates queried to determine an output.']
- **limit-reporting-independence-and-conventions** (hypothesis): The refined and coarse error bounds assume independent centered variance-one inputs. Their marginal laws may differ, but the report must preserve its prescribed endpoints, selector ties, and distinct report types. Blocked use: ['Apply the omitted-coordinate error calculation to dependent inputs.', 'Change boundary conventions or merge report types while retaining the original degree and error conclusions.']
- **limit-strict-gaussian-gain-before-amplification** (hypothesis): Finite report semantics and a retained-variance lower bound do not establish amplification. The construction additionally needs a strict positive Gaussian saving that survives transfer to the approximating laws. Blocked use: ['Use arbitrary intervals and a selector as though they necessarily produce a positive variance gain.', 'Present finite report enumeration as verification of the universal counterexample theorem.']
- **limit-stage-dependent-finite-counts** (nonconstructive): The proof establishes finite choices through limiting arguments. Each amplification stage chooses its own sufficiently large copy count for its current observation; this does not supply a uniform resource bound for the complete construction. Blocked use: ['Claim an explicit efficient counterexample generator from the existence proof.', 'Reuse a copy count or moment bound from an earlier observation without verifying the current stage.']
- **limit-switching-positive-event-probability** (hypothesis): The switching construction conditions on its exceptional event only after establishing positive probability. Its gain also requires the added score to be independent of the original tuple. Blocked use: ['Define conditional event means or variances when the event has zero probability.', 'Replace the added independent score with a correlated coordinate in the switching identities.']
- **limit-refinement-actual-saving-and-boundaries** (hypothesis): Complement refinement requires a positive-probability saving event with Gaussian-null boundary and the prescribed pointwise coordinate decomposition. The grid approximation error must be smaller than the actual probability-weighted variance saving. Blocked use: ['Choose an arbitrary coarse grid and assume that retained variance still exceeds average cost.', 'Refine an event lacking positive probability, strict conditional-variance saving, or Gaussian-null boundary.']
- **limit-fixed-partition-strict-mean-cost-advantage** (hypothesis): Mean-cost amplification requires a fixed finite partition, positive integer label costs in the stated range, pointwise coordinate bounds, Gaussian-null cell boundaries, and retained variance strictly exceeding mean cost. Blocked use: ['Invoke amplification when retained variance equals mean cost.', 'Change the partition during a transfer limit without proving a replacement transfer argument.']
- **limit-zero-gaussian-cells-and-lower-limit** (edge_case): Cells of zero Gaussian probability remain pointwise cells because discrete approximating laws may charge them. Fixed-partition transfer gives a lower-limit bound for retained variance, rather than a general convergence equality. Blocked use: ['Delete Gaussian-null cells before applying the partition to discrete inputs.', 'Replace the retained-variance lower-limit inequality with convergence equality.']
- **limit-typical-word-merging-and-variance-loss** (approximation): The common degree bound applies to both retained words and the merged failure cell through the complement identity. Small failure probability must also be combined with the current finite score's moment control to bound variance loss. Blocked use: ['Use the average label cost as an everywhere-valid degree bound before merging uncontrolled-cost words.', 'Conclude negligible variance loss solely from a small failure probability.']
- **limit-weighted-mixture-conditional-weights** (hypothesis): The weighted-mixture variance calculation uses bin probabilities conditioned on the exceptional event. The original bin probabilities cannot replace those weights, and the saving is asserted only after a sufficiently large finite truncation parameter is chosen. Blocked use: ['Compute the event-conditioned variance using unconditioned bin weights.', 'Claim the conditional-variance saving for every positive truncation parameter or claim uniform batch sizes in that parameter.']
- **limit-small-width-moving-coefficients** (approximation): The small-width transfer first fixes the intervals and selector. Its law-dependent predictor coefficients require convergence of interval probabilities and moments together with restricted selector-cell moments; coefficient convergence alone is insufficient. Blocked use: ['Transfer the law-dependent error bound solely from convergence of predictor coefficients.', 'Ignore inverse interval probabilities or vary the width during the fixed-parameter transfer argument.']

          "quote": "Let $q\\ge2$, let $\\mathcal P:\\mathbb R^q\\to\\mathcal L$ be a measurable map to a finite label set, and let $\\ell:\\mathcal L\\to\\{1,\\ldots,q\\}$. Suppose that, on every product of finite input alphabets, each cell indicator for $l$ is a finite linear combination of functions of at most $\\ell(l)$ input coordinates. Suppose also that every cell has boundary of standard Gaussian measure zero in $\\mathbb R^q$. For independent standard normals $G_1,\\ldots,G_q$, put $$S_G=\\sum_{i=1}^qG_i,\\qquad\n V=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(\\mathbb E[S_G\\mid\\mathcal P(G)]),\\qquad\n c=\\mathbb E\\ell(\\mathcal P(G)).$$ If $V>c$, then finite observations on sign cubes have arbitrarily large ratios $v(F)/D$, where $D>0$ is a cell degree bound. The Boolean conclusion of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a> follows by taking the sign of the final score, without a further averaging step. All partitions and observations are defined pointwise, including cells of zero Gaussian probability.",
          "quote_sha256": "9bd9cabb9fb9f47a0f2c045b76a08cc19bb15dc0586dcdd282b1b1bca722e03b"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-mc-transfer-2",
          "quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-lem-mc-transfer</summary>\n\n\n*Proof of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:mean-cost\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:mean-cost\">9</a>.* The partition and costs are fixed throughout the proof. Choose $$0<\\delta<(V-c)/2,\n \\qquad 1<\\lambda<\\frac{V-\\delta}{c+\\delta}.$$ For any centered, variance-one law $Z$, use independent copies to define $$S_Z=\\sum_{i=1}^q Z_i,\\qquad L=\\mathcal P(\\mathbf Z),\\qquad\n V_Z=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}(\\mathbb E[S_Z\\mid L]),\\qquad c_Z=\\mathbb E\\ell(L).$$ For a finite observation $F$ with $v=v(F)>0$, write $Z=T_F/\\sqrt v$. We will increase $v/D$ while maintaining $$\\label{eq:invariant}\n V_Z>V-\\delta,\\qquad c_Z<c+\\delta/2,\\qquad \\mathbb E|Z|>1/2.$$ Lemma <a href=\"#lem:mc-transfer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:mc-transfer\">10</a> shows that all three inequalities hold eventually along every sequence of centered, variance-one laws converging to a standard normal.\n\nTo initialize, observe the bit sum itself: $F=H_N$, with score $T_F=H_N$ and $v=D=N$. The central limit theorem for independent uniform signs [[Durrett](#ref-Durrett), Theorem 3.4.1] supplies a finite $N$ for which <a href=\"#eq:invariant\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:invariant\">[eq:invariant]</a> holds. The initial ratio is $v/D=1$.\n\n\nNow hold one such $F$ fixed. Apply $\\mathcal P$ to the standardized scores of $q$ independent copies of $F$, obtaining the group label $L$. The algebraic assumption and the degree calculus give $$\\deg(\\mathbf 1_{\\{L=l\\}})\\le D\\ell(l).$$ The tower property identifies the group score and its variance as $$\\label{eq:group}\n T_L=\\sqrt v\\,\\mathbb E[S_Z\\mid L],\\qquad\n \\tau^2:=v(L)=vV_Z>0.$$ Thus this group has a variance advantage relative to its mean cost. We next obtain a common degree bound without losing that advantage.\n\n\nTake $K$ independent copies of $L$. Retain a label word $(l_1,\\ldots,l_K)$ when $\\sum_{b=1}^K\\ell(l_b)\\le K(c+\\delta)$. Let $F'_K$ report each retained word exactly and report one failure label for every other word. Every retained-word indicator is a product with degree at most $$\\label{eq:degree}\n D'_K=DK(c+\\delta).$$ The failure indicator is one minus the finite sum of all retained-word indicators, so it satisfies the same degree bound on every input. The real upper bound $D'_K$ need not be rounded to an integer. Since the independent costs have mean $c_Z<c+\\delta/2$, the law of large numbers [[Durrett](#ref-Durrett), Theorem 2.2.3] gives a failure probability $\\varepsilon_K\\to0$.\n\n\nWrite $U_K=\\sum_{b=1}^KT_{L_b}$ and $T'_K=T_{F'_K}$. The tower property again gives $T'_K=\\mathbb E[U_K\\mid F'_K]$. On a retained word, $U_K$ is known exactly. On failure, its conditional mean is at least as good a predictor as zero [[Durrett](#ref-Durrett), Theorem 4.1.15]. Consequently $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E(U_K-T'_K)^2\n &\\le\\mathbb E[U_K^2\\mathbf 1_{\\{\\mathrm{failure}\\}}]\\notag\\\\\n &\\le(\\mathbb EU_K^4)^{1/2}\\varepsilon_K^{1/2}\n   =o(K\\tau^2). \\label{eq:loss}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For the last equality, $T_L$ has finite range, mean zero, and positive variance, and independence gives $$\\mathbb EU_K^4=K\\mathbb ET_L^4+3K(K-1)\\tau^4=O(K^2\\tau^4).$$ The implicit constant can depend on the current observation, which has been fixed before $K$ tends to infinity.\n\nThe conditional-expectation projection identity [[Durrett](#ref-Durrett), Theorem 4.1.15 and its proof] now yields $$v(F'_K)=K\\tau^2-\\mathbb E(U_K-T'_K)^2\\sim K\\tau^2.$$ In particular, this variance is positive for all sufficiently large $K$. The central limit theorem [[Durrett](#ref-Durrett), Theorem 3.4.1] gives $U_K/(\\sqrt K\\tau)\\Rightarrow N(0,1)$. By <a href=\"#eq:loss\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:loss\">[eq:loss]</a>, replacing $U_K$ by $T'_K$ changes the normalized variable by a term tending to zero in $L^2$. Moreover, $\\sqrt{v(F'_K)}/(\\sqrt K\\tau)\\to1$. The addition and rescaling forms of Slutsky’s theorem [[Durrett](#ref-Durrett), Exercises 3.2.13–3.2.14] therefore give $$\\frac{T'_K}{\\sqrt{v(F'_K)}}\\Rightarrow N(0,1).$$ These new standardized scores are centered and have variance one. Lemma <a href=\"#lem:mc-transfer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:mc-transfer\">10</a> restores all three inequalities in <a href=\"#eq:invariant\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:invariant\">[eq:invariant]</a> for sufficiently large $K$. At the same time, <a href=\"#eq:group\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:group\">[eq:group]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:degree\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:degree\">[eq:degree]</a> give $$\\frac{v(F'_K)}{D'_K}\\longrightarrow\n \\frac vD\\frac{V_Z}{c+\\delta}>\\lambda\\frac vD.$$ One sufficiently large finite $K$ therefore restores every maintained condition and increases the ratio by a factor greater than $\\lambda$.\n\nFor any prescribed $C>0$, choose a positive integer $k$ with $\\lambda^k>4C^2$. Perform $k$ such steps, choosing a finite batch size separately at each step. The final observation remains finite and satisfies $$v(F)/D>4C^2,\\qquad \\mathbb E|T_F|>\\tfrac12\\sqrt{v(F)}.$$ Let $N$ now denote the total number of bits underlying the final observation. Its sign readout $f=\\operatorname{sign}(T_F)$, with sign $1$ at zero, has degree at most $D$. Conditioning the bit sum on $F$ gives the signed identity $$\\sum_i\\widehat f(\\{i\\})=\\mathbb E[fH_N]=\\mathbb E|T_F|\n >\\tfrac12\\sqrt{v(F)}>C\\sqrt D\\ge C\\sqrt{\\deg(f)}.$$ The positive signed correlation excludes constant $f$. Arbitrarily large target ratios follow from the same finite iteration. ◻",
          "quote_sha256": "bf60cad5bb01be875065259f488cf41705ae1b1ad502d8b21aea9b57656bf1c9"
        }

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 192 (`lean/docs/192.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Unbounded Violations of the Square-Root Degree Bound](../../preprints/Unbounded-Violations-of-the-Square-Root-Degree-Bound-September-26-2026/Unbounded-Violations-of-the-Square-Root-Degree-Bound-September-26-2026.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization disproves the Gopalan–Servedio square-root degree conjecture by an unbounded factor. For every $C>0$, it gives a nonconstant Boolean function on a finite sign cube for which the sum of its linear Fourier coefficients exceeds $C\sqrt{\deg f}$, where $\deg f$ is its real multilinear degree.
>
> It also proves that the ratio of the sum of the absolute values of those coefficients to $\sqrt{\deg f}$ is unbounded among positive-degree Boolean functions.

Comparator source `SquareRootDegree.lean` (1944 bytes, sha256 `72f48e513f3159bf80d25c7ed5afee9f3224f5c09c672777ead3f1ec7309ac4f`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

/-!
Bool encodes a sign: false is +1 and true is -1. The average is uniform,
and degree is ordinary real Fourier degree, not F₂ degree or threshold degree.
-/

open scoped BigOperators

noncomputable section

namespace SquareRootDegree

abbrev Cube (ι : Type*) := ι → Bool

def sign (b : Bool) : ℝ := if b then -1 else 1

def average {α : Type*} [Fintype α] (f : α → ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (Fintype.card α : ℝ)⁻¹ * ∑ x, f x

def character {ι : Type*} (s : Finset ι) (x : Cube ι) : ℝ :=
  ∏ i ∈ s, sign (x i)

def fourierCoeff {ι : Type*} [Fintype ι] (f : Cube ι → ℝ) (s : Finset ι) : ℝ := by
  classical
  exact average (fun x => f x * character s x)

def fourierDegree {ι : Type*} [Fintype ι] (f : Cube ι → ℝ) : ℕ := by
  classical
  exact (Finset.univ.filter (fun s : Finset ι => fourierCoeff f s ≠ 0)).sup Finset.card

def IsBoolean {α : Type*} (f : α → ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ x, f x = -1 ∨ f x = 1

def Nonconstant {α : Type*} (f : α → ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∃ x y, f x ≠ f y

def singletonSum {ι : Type*} [Fintype ι] (f : Cube ι → ℝ) : ℝ :=
  ∑ i, fourierCoeff f {i}

def absoluteSingletonSum {ι : Type*} [Fintype ι] (f : Cube ι → ℝ) : ℝ :=
  ∑ i, |fourierCoeff f {i}|

def SignedViolations : Prop :=
  ∀ C : ℝ, 0 < C → ∃ n : ℕ, 0 < n ∧ ∃ f : Cube (Fin n) → ℝ,
    IsBoolean f ∧ Nonconstant f ∧ C * Real.sqrt (fourierDegree f) < singletonSum f

def AbsoluteRatios : Set ℝ :=
  {r | ∃ n : ℕ, 0 < n ∧ ∃ f : Cube (Fin n) → ℝ,
    IsBoolean f ∧ 0 < fourierDegree f ∧
      r = absoluteSingletonSum f / Real.sqrt (fourierDegree f)}

/-- The two clauses of the literal main theorem. Unboundedness above is the
real-valued formulation of the displayed supremum being positive infinity. -/
theorem main : SignedViolations ∧ ¬ BddAbove AbsoluteRatios := by
  sorry

end SquareRootDegree

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We disprove the Gopalan–Servedio square-root conjecture, even up to an arbitrary constant factor. For every real <i>C</i> &gt; 0, there is a nonconstant Boolean function $`f:\{-1,1\}^n\to\{-1,1\}`$ on a finite sign cube such that

$`\displaystyle \sum_{i=1}^n \widehat f(\{i\})\gt C\sqrt{\deg(f)}.`$

Here $`\widehat f(\{i\})`$ is the linear Fourier coefficient associated with the <i>i</i>th input, and $`\deg(f)`$ is the degree of the real multilinear polynomial representing <i>f</i>.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
